12
Condensed and perfectoid AdS/CFT

Let
M
be an asymptotically AdS
d+1
bulk with conformal boundary data
\partial M
, let
\{\partial M_{\Lambda}\}_{\Lambda}
denote a cofinal inverse system of cutoff hypersurfaces with restriction maps
r_{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}:\partial M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}\to\partial M_{\Lambda}
for
\Lambda^{\prime}>\Lambda
, and write
\partial M_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{\Lambda}\partial M_{\Lambda}
as the limit background in the pro-category. The cutoff family and its limit are the definition-level version of holographic RG, in which local bulk dynamics is unchanged while boundary data is assembled as a compatible system across cutoffs. Why old methods were definitionally fragile the usual presentation treats
\Lambda\to\infty
as an analytic limit on functionals without specifying a category in which boundary fields and their global sectors form an exact descent object on
\partial M_{\infty}
. We treat
\partial M_{\infty}
as a condensed space and boundary data as condensed sheaves/modules
\mathcal{F}
on the relevant profinite/pro-site, so that sections are defined by exact descent and inverse limits are controlled homologically
[
6
]
. This supplies a canonical notion of boundary fields and global sectors on inverse-limit backgrounds, which is exactly what duality-rich AdS/CFT needs.

Why old methods were definitionally fragile, without condensed exactness, taking limits of cutoff data does not commute with taking sections/observables, and scheme choices become inseparable from the definition of the background. We then organize non-Archimedean avatars via perfectoid tilting, treating a tower-completed boundary carrier
X
as a perfectoid object with tilt
X^{\flat}

[
32
]
, and we regard the
p
-adic AdS/CFT constructions as arithmetic avatars compatible with this categorical bridge
[
22
,
4
]
. Tilting provides a principled candidate mechanism relating Archimedean and
p
-adic holography at the level of tower-completed boundary data. Why old methods were definitionally fragile, classical AdS/CFT has no canonical operation that relates distinct analytic avatars of a single inverse-limit boundary object. The only theorem-level input used here is the exactness/descent formalism of condensed sheaves and the invariant computations already established in earlier sections, any strong claims about full reconstruction theorems or dynamical equivalences across tilts are stated as Program.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an abelian category and
\{A_{\Lambda}\}_{\Lambda}
an inverse system in
\mathcal{A}
, inverse limit
\varprojlim
is left exact but not exact in general, with derived functors
\varprojlim{}^{\,i}
measuring the failure, and one has the standard exact sequence

0\to\varprojlim A_{\Lambda}\to\prod_{\Lambda}A_{\Lambda}\xrightarrow{1-\mathrm{shift}}\prod_{\Lambda}A_{\Lambda}\to\varprojlim{}^{\,1}A_{\Lambda}\to 0,

(216)

where
(1-\mathrm{shift})(a_{\Lambda})=(a_{\Lambda}-f_{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}(a_{\Lambda^{\prime}}))
. The appearance of
\varprojlim{}^{\,1}
is the precise obstruction to defining renormalized boundary observables as naive limits of cutoff observables. Why old methods were definitionally fragile standard AdS/CFT manipulates
\Lambda\to\infty
limits of correlators and counterterms in a way that implicitly assumes exactness of
\varprojlim
on the relevant spaces of data, which is false in general and becomes acute in tower/inverse-limit settings. For a presheaf or sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on the cutoff family, writing
A_{\Lambda}=\Gamma(\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F})
, the failure of commutation between “take sections” and “take limits” is precisely the failure of the canonical map

\Gamma\!\left(\varprojlim_{\Lambda}\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F}\right)\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{\Lambda}\Gamma(\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F})

(217)

to be an isomorphism, with the obstruction governed by a
\varprojlim{}^{\,1}
term in the appropriate derived setting. The correct definition-level boundary datum is not the naive compatible limit of cutoff sections, but the derived limit that remembers extension data between scales. Why old methods were definitionally fragile the usual renormalization narrative treats scheme dependence as a choice of local counterterms but does not isolate the categorical obstruction that scheme dependence is precisely the ambiguity in splitting an extension encoded by
\varprojlim{}^{\,1}
. In duality-rich AdS/CFT, global sectors and line operators require tracking discrete data (global form of the gauge group, higher-form symmetries, torsion flux sectors), which are naturally classified by generalized cohomology rather than by local field configurations, so boundary data must live in a homologically controlled category in which these sectors glue across cutoffs.

Global-sector bookkeeping is part of the definition of the boundary theory, not optional decoration, and it must be compatible with duality actions on charge lattices. Why old methods were definitionally fragile, classical formulations often bolt on global-form and higher-form symmetry data after defining the theory on a manifold boundary, whereas inverse-limit boundaries and arithmetic monodromies force these sectors to be intrinsic background data. Finally, tower completions can produce boundary carriers that are not manifolds (e.g. solenoids), so the manifold-based axiomatics of QFT do not even specify what it means to put the CFT on the limit background without enlarging the category of spaces. The correct boundary “space” in such settings is a sheaf-theoretic object defined by descent from finite stages, not a manifold. Why old methods were definitionally fragile the standard AdS/CFT dictionary assumes a smooth conformal boundary and therefore cannot treat inverse-limit boundary carriers as a single background statement, only as a family of unrelated approximations.

Let
\mathrm{Prof}
be the site of profinite sets with the usual Grothendieck topology and let
\mathrm{Cond}
denote the category of condensed sets
[
6
]
, for an inverse-limit boundary carrier
X=\varprojlim_{\Lambda}X_{\Lambda}
arising from a tower or cutoff system, we regard
X
as a condensed space and specify boundary source/operator data as a condensed module
\mathcal{M}
over a condensed ring
\mathcal{O}_{X}
, so that sources are sections
J\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M})
and operator insertions are linear functionals on
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M})
in the appropriate topological sense. Boundary couplings and operator sources are promoted to descent data on a genuine inverse-limit background, mirroring how stacks are the correct home for quotient geometries. Why old methods were definitionally fragile on inverse-limit boundaries the naive notion of function space does not have exact descent, so one cannot define sources and global sectors as honest background data without the condensed enlargement. The sheaf condition on
\mathcal{M}
is the exactness requirement that for any profinite cover
\{U_{i}\to U\}
one has

0\to\mathcal{M}(U)\to\prod_{i}\mathcal{M}(U_{i})\rightrightarrows\prod_{i,j}\mathcal{M}(U_{i}\times_{U}U_{j})

(218)

exact, and we impose this as the definition-level gluing condition for boundary data across refinements, overlaps, and cutoff families. Eq.(
218
) is the categorical form of “boundary data is globally defined if and only if it is compatible locally,” now applied to tower/cutoff covers rather than to manifold charts. Why old methods were definitionally fragile ordinary sheaf theory on topological spaces does not behave stably under inverse limits in the categories relevant for QFT observables, so compatibility across cutoffs does not automatically define a unique global object.

Let
M
admit a radial function
r
with cutoff hypersurfaces
\partial M_{\Lambda}=\{r=\Lambda\}
and restriction maps
r_{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}:\partial M_{\Lambda^{\prime}}\to\partial M_{\Lambda}
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf of source data (e.g. boundary values of bulk fields) so that
A_{\Lambda}:=\Gamma(\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F})
forms an inverse system in an abelian category. The fundamental obstruction in the naive formulation is that
\varprojlim
is not exact, so the naive “renormalized source space”
\varprojlim_{\Lambda}A_{\Lambda}
need not coincide with
\Gamma(\varprojlim_{\Lambda}\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F})
, and in derived form one expects an exact sequence of Milnor type,

0\to\varprojlim{}^{\,1}_{\Lambda}\Gamma(\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F}_{-1})\to\Gamma\!\left(\varprojlim_{\Lambda}\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F}\right)\to\varprojlim_{\Lambda}\Gamma(\partial M_{\Lambda},\mathcal{F})\to 0,

(219)

whose nontrivial
\varprojlim{}^{\,1}
term measures the failure of naive limit/section commutation. Eq.(
219
) is the definition-level explanation of why renormalized boundary data and counterterms cannot be treated as purely analytic limits on functionals without specifying the categorical exactness structure. Why old methods were definitionally fragile the standard holographic renormalization procedure defines counterterms by asymptotic analysis but does not isolate the extension ambiguity encoded by
\varprojlim{}^{\,1}
, which is precisely what becomes unavoidable in tower/inverse-limit settings. A concrete obstruction is exhibited by an inverse system of abelian groups
A_{n}=\mathbb{Z}
with bonding maps
f_{n}:A_{n+1}\to A_{n}
,
f_{n}(a)=pa
, for which

\varprojlim_{n}(\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\times p}\cdots)=0,

(220)

while
\varprojlim{}^{\,1}
is nontrivial, as follows from (
216
) by taking the element
s=(1,1,1,\ldots)\in\prod_{n}\mathbb{Z}
and observing that the equation
s=(1-\mathrm{shift})(t)
, i.e.
1=t_{n}-pt_{n+1}
for all
n
, has no solution
t_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
because iterating gives
t_{n}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p^{k})
for all
k
and hence forces
t_{n}
to have a nonterminating
p
-adic expansion not realized by any integer. Eq.(

) together with the explicit nontriviality argument shows that naive inverse limits can erase data while a derived obstruction survives, which is the categorical shadow of scheme dependence and extension ambiguity in renormalization. 14
Conclusion

Traditional F-theory encodes the Type IIB axio-dilaton
\tau
as elliptic-fiber data but reaches the ten-dimensional description through a shrinking-fiber limit in which the M-theory torus area is sent to zero at fixed complex structure, a step that is physically powerful yet definitionally singular from the eleven-dimensional viewpoint
[
39
,
29
,
28
,
8
]
. In this work we replace that singular slogan by a non-singular tower-completed compactification carrier, the perfectoid circle
S_{f}^{1}
, interpreted in the condensed framework so that fields and global sectors on the inverse-limit object are defined by exact descent
[
6
,
32
]
. The tilt
\to
untilt/comparison
\to
analytification machinery supplies an elliptic-curve datum
\mathcal{E}
as an output of the tower-completed carrier, thereby providing a definition-level geometric home for modular data without postulating a literal smooth twelve-dimensional spacetime
[
32
,
31
]
. The constant-
\tau
sector is then established at the level explicitly checked in the paper
\tau=C_{0}+ie^{-\phi}
is matched to M-theory radius/metric data and
C^{(3)}
holonomy data, the protected
(p,q)
spectrum is reproduced by wrapped M2 sectors, and the low-energy effective action matches the constant-
\tau
Type IIB supergravity action including the required topological couplings
[
34
,
33
]
. The modular organization realized geometrically in the construction is the profinite/congruence-level structure explicitly carried by the tower, while the completion to the full quantum modular group and the incorporation of fermionic/anomaly refinements are treated as future work. The net result is a definition-level replacement of the singular shrinking-fiber prescription by an honest compactification object whose local dynamics remains standard supergravity while the global sectors and modular bookkeeping are intrinsic.

First, the shrinking-fiber prescription is replaced by an honest compactification carrier
S_{f}^{1}=\varprojlim(S^{1}\xleftarrow{z\mapsto z^{p}}S^{1}\xleftarrow{}\cdots)
, so the modular datum is tied to a non-singular object rather than to a degenerating cycle, and this is precisely what the manifold-based shrinking-torus formulation cannot supply as a background statement because the carrier disappears in the limit. Second, the elliptic fiber datum is produced by the tower-completed geometry through the tilt/untilt/analytification machinery, so elliptic data becomes an output of compactification geometry rather than an auxiliary input, and this avoids the definition-level gap in which
\tau
survives a collapse that removes the underlying cycle. Third, the constant-
\tau
dictionary is made explicit
\mathrm{Im}\,\tau
is fixed by the M-theory radius/metric modulus and
\mathrm{Re}\,\tau=C_{0}
by a
C^{(3)}
holonomy sector, so
\tau
is determined by eleven-dimensional data rather than postulated, whereas in the shrinking-fiber formulation
\tau
is introduced as a complex-structure parameter that is kept physical while the fiber is removed. Fourth, the protected
(p,q)
tower is recovered from wrapped M2 sectors in the tower-completed geometry with the correct
|p+q\tau|
dependence at the level checked, and the quantization/anomaly constraints selecting the physically allowed integral lattice are treated as part of the global-sector bookkeeping, whereas the conventional approach identifies
(p,q)
sectors through the auxiliary torus and does not provide a non-singular eleven-dimensional carrier in which the same organization is intrinsic.

Fifth, the low-energy effective action matching is carried out explicitly in the constant-
\tau
bosonic sector, including the axion/topological couplings inherited from the eleven-dimensional Chern-Simons term, and this is a stringent check that goes beyond spectrum matching, whereas a slogan-level definition of F-theory does not by itself provide a controlled reduction on a well-defined carrier. Sixth, the geometric organization of modular data is made intrinsic to the tower-completed carrier at the profinite/congruence level explicitly derived from the tower, which is precisely the level at which the geometry is proven to carry arithmetic structure without overclaiming a full quantum
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
completion. Seventh, tower completion makes canonical the refinement of global holonomy/Fourier sectors through invariants such as
\widehat{\Sigma_{p}}\cong\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
on inverse-limit boundary carriers and their higher-rank generalizations, which cannot be expressed as a single background invariant in manifold-only language because the inverse-limit carrier is not a manifold and requires descent to define fields. Eighth, the role of generalized cohomology becomes structural rather than decorative inverse-limit/tower backgrounds naturally generate torsion/completion sectors visible through
K
-theoretic and derived-limit data, aligning with the string-theoretic principle that globally quantized sectors are not faithfully captured by naive homology on profinite objects, whereas the shrinking-fiber approach tends to treat such sectors as external discrete choices.

Ninth, varying-
\tau
backgrounds are formulated definition-level as perfectoid-circle fibrations
\pi:\mathcal{X}\to B\setminus\Delta
whose elliptic fibration and monodromy arise intrinsically from the fibred tower-completed carrier, and the explicit local
T
-monodromy
\tau\mapsto\tau+1
is derived from
q=\exp(2\pi i\tau)
in the tower-compatible Tate uniformization picture, whereas in conventional treatments monodromy is specified as auxiliary fibration data tied to a singular collapse. Tenth, the same formalism admits a precise higher-rank generalization in which
\tau
is replaced by a period matrix
\Omega\in\mathfrak{H}_{r}
and arithmetic monodromy acts by
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{Z})
on an integral charge lattice
\Gamma\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{2r}
, providing a definition-level framework for U-duality-type geometry that is not naturally packageable within rank-one elliptic-fiber language. Eleventh, the AdS/CFT interpretation becomes definition-level cleaner duality actions on boundary couplings and Wilson-’t Hooft lattices are tied to intrinsic monodromy of bulk tower-completed compactification data, and tower-completed boundary carriers provide canonical invariants for global-sector bookkeeping, whereas the manifold-only boundary formalism treats such refinements as external to the background. Twelfth, the adelic viewpoint is made a well-posed completion principle the prime
p
is bookkeeping for the tower and the natural global object is an adelic assembly over all primes together with the Archimedean place, which is consistent with the arithmetic nature of duality groups and with the fact that single-prime refinements should not define distinct physics, even though the full adelic completion remains programmatic.

Inverse limits and refinement towers are not manifolds in general, and ordinary topology and naive sheaf theory are fragile under inverse limits precisely in the ways that define physical backgrounds gluing, exactness, and homological control are what enforce flux quantization, discrete holonomies, and anomaly constraints, and these operations do not behave well in the classical category of spaces when one passes to tower limits. Condensed mathematics is the minimal enlargement in which profinite and inverse-limit objects admit well-behaved sheaves defined by exact descent, so that “fields on the limit” are definition-level compatible families on finite stages rather than heuristic limiting prescriptions
[
6
]
. Perfectoid geometry is the minimal tower-complete analytic geometry in which such towers admit a tilting equivalence, and tilting supplies the bridge from tower-completed geometry to elliptic data without taking any singular shrinking limit
[
32
,
31
]
. Condensed objects play for inverse-limit compactifications the same role that stacks play for gauge quotients they are not optional language, but the minimal correction that makes the background definition-level well-posed.

The constant-
\tau
sector established here should be viewed as the controlled base case of a definition-level F-theory framework the modular datum is produced from a non-singular compactification carrier,
\tau
is fixed by an eleven-dimensional radius/holonomy dictionary, and protected spectra and low-energy couplings match the constant-
\tau
Type IIB sector at the level explicitly computed. The immediate implication is that F-theory can be formulated without appealing to the singular shrinking-fiber slogan as a definition, while retaining the physical content that makes F-theory useful in practice. The varying-
\tau
generalization is then naturally formulated as perfectoid-circle fibrations over a base, with 7-branes realized as intrinsic monodromy/defect data of the fibred tower-completed carrier rather than as external branch-cut prescriptions, this is definition-level well-posed within the framework but is programmatic beyond the local models and monodromy derivations presented here. In this sense, the construction upgrades F-theory from an extraordinarily effective limiting prescription to a framework in which the definition problem is replaced by a concrete compactification object whose global sectors and modular organization are intrinsic and whose extensions can be posed as geometric and cohomological questions rather than as heuristic limits.

AdS/CFT is fundamentally sensitive to global sectors-line operators, higher-form symmetries, discrete theta data-and to exact duality actions implemented by defects and interfaces, and these are precisely the structures that the tower-completed/condensed framework packages canonically as intrinsic background data. The tower-completed boundary carriers supply computable invariants for holonomy/Fourier sectors and refine the bookkeeping of line-operator sectors in a way that is not expressible as a single background statement in a manifold-only boundary formalism, while the bulk monodromy derived from fibred tower-completed compactification data provides a definition-level origin for duality interfaces acting on boundary couplings and charge lattices. The tilting perspective suggests, as a program with checkable milestones, a principled bridge between Archimedean and
p
-adic holography in which
p
-adic AdS/CFT arises as an arithmetic avatar of tower-completed boundary data transported by tilting
[
22
,
4
]
, this is not claimed as a theorem here, but the present framework makes the comparison problem definition-level well-posed because both avatars are treated as different geometric realizations of the same tower data.

The same definition-level philosophy extrapolates naturally beyond rank-one F-theory instead of geometrizing only the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
action on
\tau
and the
(p,q)
lattice via an elliptic curve, one replaces elliptic data by higher-rank perfectoid/condensed group objects whose period data is encoded by
\Omega\in\mathfrak{H}_{r}
and whose intrinsic arithmetic monodromy acts by
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{Z})
(or an appropriate arithmetic subgroup) on an integral charge lattice
\Gamma\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{2r}
, thereby geometrizing U-duality-type mixing of multiple brane-charge sectors. This is not an ornamental generalization it is the minimal higher-rank analogue of what F-theory already does in rank one, and it becomes natural once one insists that duality actions are arithmetic actions on quantum charge lattices and that tower completions and global-sector constraints are intrinsic to the compactification carrier. The adelic perspective is the corresponding completion principle single-prime tower constructions are local charts, and a global arithmetic duality geometry should assemble all primes together with the Archimedean factor, so that physically meaningful invariants are formulated in a way that is independent of the bookkeeping choice of
p
and is compatible with the arithmetic nature of duality groups.
Perfectoid and condensed geometry provide the missing definition-level carrier for the modular data that F-theory exploits, replacing a singular shrinking limit by a genuine tower-completed compactification object and opening a systematic route to arithmetic formulations of duality and holography consistent with protected spectra and effective actions.

Appendix A
Tower invariants of the solenoidal circle

Fix a prime
p
. Let

\mathbb{T}:=S^{1}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ |z|=1\}

(244)

with group law given by multiplication. Define the degree-
p
endomorphism

f:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{T},\qquad f(z)=z^{p}.

(245)

Let
\bigl(\mathbb{T}_{n},f_{n}\bigr)_{n\geq 0}
denote the constant tower
(\mathbb{T}_{n}=\mathbb{T})
with bonding maps
(f_{n}=f:\mathbb{T}_{n+1}\to\mathbb{T}_{n})
.

Define the inverse limit in the category
\mathbf{CompHausGrp}
of compact Hausdorff abelian groups:

\Sigma_{p}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,(\mathbb{T}_{n},f_{n})=\Bigl\{(z_{0},z_{1},z_{2},\dots)\in\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{T}:\ z_{n}=z_{n+1}^{\,p}\ \forall n\Bigr\},

(246)

with coordinatewise multiplication. Let

\pi_{n}:\Sigma_{p}\to\mathbb{T},\qquad\pi_{n}\bigl((z_{m})_{m\geq 0}\bigr)=z_{n},

(247)

so that

\pi_{n}=f\circ\pi_{n+1},\qquad\forall n\geq 0.

(248)

Let
\mathbf{Prof}
denote the category of profinite sets. For any compact Hausdorff space
X
, write the associated functor

\underline{X}:\mathbf{Prof}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{Sets},\qquad\underline{X}(S):=\mathrm{Cont}(S,X).

(249)

(Thus
\underline{X}
is the standard “condensed” avatar of
X
obtained by restricting the Yoneda embedding to
\mathbf{Prof}
.)

The purpose of this appendix is to compute, for
\Sigma_{p}
, the following tower-stable invariants and to record (in a single place) the non-identities among them:

\pi_{1}^{(p)}\text{-data}\quad,\quad\check{H}^{1}(\Sigma_{p};\mathbb{Z})\quad,\quad\Sigma_{p}^{\vee}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Sigma_{p},\mathbb{T})\quad,\quad\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}\text{-terms in }K^{*}(\Sigma_{p}).

(250)

Lemma A.1

(universal property, explicit equalizer.)
.

For every compact Hausdorff space
S
,

\mathrm{Cont}(S,\Sigma_{p})\;\cong\;\Bigl\{(\varphi_{n})_{n\geq 0}\in\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Cont}(S,\mathbb{T}):\ \varphi_{n}=f\circ\varphi_{n+1}\ \forall n\Bigr\}.

(251)

Proof.

A map
\varphi:S\to\Sigma_{p}\subseteq\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{T}
is equivalent to a family

\varphi_{n}:=\pi_{n}\circ\varphi\in\mathrm{Cont}(S,\mathbb{T})

satisfying
\varphi_{n}=f\circ\varphi_{n+1}
by (
248
).
Conversely, any such compatible family defines
\varphi(s):=(\varphi_{n}(s))_{n\geq 0}\in\Sigma_{p}
.
Continuity follows from the product topology.
\square

Corollary A.2

(Evaluation in
\mathbf{Prof}
tower-descent formula.)
.

For every profinite set
S
,

\underline{\Sigma}_{p}(S)=\mathrm{Cont}(S,\Sigma_{p})\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Cont}(S,\mathbb{T}),\qquad\text{with transition maps }(\psi\mapsto f\circ\psi).

(252)

Equivalently, in functor form,

\underline{\Sigma}_{p}\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,\underline{\mathbb{T}}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathbf{Fun}(\mathbf{Prof}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{Sets}).

(253)

Let
\mu_{p^{n}}\subset\mathbb{T}
denote the cyclic subgroup of
p^{n}
-th roots of unity:

\mu_{p^{n}}:=\{z\in\mathbb{T}:\ z^{p^{n}}=1\}\cong\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z},

(254)

and define the inverse limit (in
\mathbf{CompHausGrp}
)

\mu_{p^{\infty}}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\mu_{p^{n}}=\Bigl\{(\zeta_{n})_{n\geq 1}\in\prod_{n\geq 1}\mu_{p^{n}}:\ \zeta_{n}=\zeta_{n+1}^{\,p}\Bigr\}.

(255)

Lemma A.3

(kernel of
\pi_{0}
).)
.

The projection
\pi_{0}:\Sigma_{p}\to\mathbb{T}
is a continuous surjective group homomorphism with kernel

\ker(\pi_{0})=\{(1,z_{1},z_{2},\dots)\in\Sigma_{p}\}\;\cong\;\mu_{p^{\infty}}.

(256)

Proof.

Surjectivity given
z_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
, choose
z_{1}\in\mathbb{T}
with
z_{1}^{p}=z_{0}
, then
z_{2}
with
z_{2}^{p}=z_{1}
, etc., producing
(z_{n})_{n\geq 0}\in\Sigma_{p}
.
Kernel condition
\pi_{0}=1
forces
z_{n}^{p^{n}}=1
, hence
z_{n}\in\mu_{p^{n}}
, and compatibility is exactly (
255
). ∎

Thus we have a short exact sequence in
\mathbf{CompHausGrp}
:

0\longrightarrow\mu_{p^{\infty}}\longrightarrow\Sigma_{p}\overset{\pi_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{T}\longrightarrow 0.

(257)

Connectedness and failure of local connectedness.

\Sigma_{p}\ \text{is compact and connected, but not locally connected and not semilocally simply connected.}

(258)

Derivation.

1.

Compactness follows from (
246
) as a closed subgroup of
\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{T}
, which is compact by Tychonoff.

2.

Each
\mathbb{T}
is connected and each
f
is surjective, hence
\Sigma_{p}=\varprojlim(\mathbb{T},f)
is connected (inverse limit of connected compact spaces with surjective bonding maps).

3.

By (
257
), every neighborhood
U\subset\mathbb{T}
of
1
has preimage
\pi_{0}^{-1}(U)
containing the totally disconnected fiber
\ker(\pi_{0})\cong\mu_{p^{\infty}}
, in particular, for any open arc
U\ni 1
,

\pi_{0}^{-1}(U)\supseteq\mu_{p^{\infty}},\qquad\text{and }\mu_{p^{\infty}}\text{ has no connected neighborhoods},

(259)

so
\Sigma_{p}
is not locally connected.

4.

For any neighborhood
V
of
1\in\Sigma_{p}
, the restriction
\pi_{0}|_{V}:V\to\pi_{0}(V)
is onto an open arc in
\mathbb{T}
, the covering maps
\pi_{n}\>\Sigma_{p}\to\mathbb{T}
produce arbitrarily high-degree “small” loops inside
V
(since
\ker(\pi_{0})
contains
p^{n}
-torsion for all
n
), hence no neighborhood has trivial fundamental group image, i.e.
\Sigma_{p}
is not semilocally simply connected:

\forall\ V\ni 1,\ \exists n\geq 1,\ \exists\gamma_{n}:S^{1}\to V,\quad[\gamma_{n}]\neq 1.

(260)

\square

Let
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbb{Z}
with generator
[{\rm loop}]
. The induced map

f_{*}:\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})\to\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T}),\qquad f_{*}(n)=pn,

(261)

follows from
\deg(f)=p
.

Define the pro-
(p)
completion of
\mathbb{Z}
by the inverse limit of finite quotients:

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\ \mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

(262)

Cover-tower
\Rightarrow
pro-
(p)
completion.

The tower of degree-
p^{n}
covers
\bigl(\mathbb{T}\overset{z\mapsto z^{p^{n}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{T}\bigr)
determines the inverse system of finite deck groups

\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})/p^{n}\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z},

(263)

hence the canonical pro-
(p)
invariant is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
as in (
262
).

Let
\check{H}^{k}(-;\mathbb{Z})
denote Čech cohomology with constant coefficients
\mathbb{Z}
. For an inverse limit of compact Hausdorff spaces with surjective bonding maps, Čech cohomology is continuous:

\check{H}^{k}\!\left(\varprojlim_{n}X_{n};\mathbb{Z}\right)\cong\varinjlim_{n}\check{H}^{k}(X_{n};\mathbb{Z}).

(264)

Apply (
264
) to
X_{n}=\mathbb{T}
and bonding
f
. Since

\check{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}\cdot\alpha,\qquad f^{*}(\alpha)=p\,\alpha,

(265)

we obtain

\check{H}^{1}(\Sigma_{p};\mathbb{Z})\cong\varinjlim\bigl(\mathbb{Z}\overset{\times p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}\overset{\times p}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\bigr)\cong\mathbb{Z}[1/p],

(266)

with the explicit identification

[(m,n)]\ \longmapsto\ \frac{m}{p^{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}[1/p],\qquad(m,n)\sim(pm,n+1).

(267)

Moreover,
\check{H}^{k}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{Z})=0
for
k>1
, so (
264
) yields

\check{H}^{k}(\Sigma_{p};\mathbb{Z})=0,\qquad k>1.

(268)

Define the tower pro-
(p)
abelian loop invariant by

H^{(p)}_{1}(\Sigma_{p};\mathbb{Z}):=\widehat{\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})}_{p}^{\,\mathrm{ab}}=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\ \pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})/p^{n}\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})\cong\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

(269)

(Here the “
\mathrm{ab}
” is redundant because
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T})
is already abelian.)

Thus we record the pair of non-equal but complementary tower invariants:

H^{(p)}_{1}(\Sigma_{p};\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p},\qquad\check{H}^{1}(\Sigma_{p};\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}[1/p].

(270)

Let

G^{\vee}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,\mathbb{T})

(271)

denote the Pontryagin dual of a compact abelian group
G
, viewed as a discrete abelian group.

Since
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}\cong\mathbb{Z}
via
\chi_{m}(z)=z^{m}
, we compute the dual map induced by
f
:

f^{\vee}:\mathbb{T}^{\vee}\to\mathbb{T}^{\vee},\qquad(\chi_{m}\mapsto\chi_{m}\circ f)=\chi_{pm}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad m\mapsto pm.

(272)

Pontryagin duality converts inverse limits of compact abelian groups with surjective bonding maps into direct limits of discrete duals:

\Bigl(\varprojlim_{n}G_{n}\Bigr)^{\vee}\cong\varinjlim_{n}G_{n}^{\vee}.

(273)

Apply (
273
) to
\Sigma_{p}=\varprojlim(\mathbb{T},f)
and (
272
):

\Sigma_{p}^{\vee}\cong\varinjlim\bigl(\mathbb{Z}\overset{\times p}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}\overset{\times p}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\bigr)\cong\mathbb{Z}[1/p].

(274)

Comparing (
274
) with (
266
) yields the canonical identification

\Sigma_{p}^{\vee}\ \cong\ \check{H}^{1}(\Sigma_{p};\mathbb{Z})\ \cong\ \mathbb{Z}[1/p].

(275)

Let
K^{*}(-)
denote complex topological
K
-theory of compact Hausdorff spaces. For an inverse limit
X=\varprojlim X_{n}
of compact Hausdorff spaces, the Milnor short exact sequence takes the form

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}K^{i-1}(X_{n})\longrightarrow K^{i}(X)\longrightarrow\varprojlim K^{i}(X_{n})\longrightarrow 0.

(276)

We apply (
276
) to
X_{n}=\mathbb{T}
,
X=\Sigma_{p}
, bonding maps
f:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{T}
.

The stage groups and induced maps are:

K^{0}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbb{Z},\qquad K^{1}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbb{Z},

(277)

f^{*}\big|_{K^{0}}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}},\qquad f^{*}\big|_{K^{1}}=\times p.

(278)

To compute
\varprojlim
and
\varprojlim^{1}
for the inverse system

\cdots\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z},

(279)

use the standard presentation (for inverse systems
(A_{n},\phi_{n})
):

\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}A_{n}\cong\mathrm{coker}\Bigl(1-\mathrm{shift}:\prod_{n\geq 0}A_{n}\to\prod_{n\geq 0}A_{n}\Bigr),\quad\mathrm{shift}\bigl((a_{n})_{n}\bigr)=\bigl(\phi_{n}(a_{n+1})\bigr)_{n}.

(280)

For (
279
),
\phi_{n}=\times p
, hence

(1-\mathrm{shift})\bigl((t_{n})_{n\geq 0}\bigr)=\bigl(t_{n}-p\,t_{n+1}\bigr)_{n\geq 0}.

(281)

The inverse limit is

\varprojlim(\cdots\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z})=\{(a_{n})_{n\geq 0}:\ a_{n}=p\,a_{n+1}\ \forall n\}=0.

(282)

Define the derived-limit group

\mathbf{T}_{p}:=\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}(\cdots\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z})\;\cong\;\Bigl(\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{Z}\Bigr)\Big/\mathrm{im}(1-\mathrm{shift}).

(283)

A standard computation identifies
\mathbf{T}_{p}
with the
p
-primary divisible quotient:

\mathbf{T}_{p}\ \cong\ \mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

(284)

(Equivalently, (
284
) fixes the canonical target group for the Milnor
\varprojlim^{1}
-obstruction attached to the tower (
279
).)
Now apply (
276
) in degrees
i=0,1
.

•

For
i=0
:

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}K^{-1}(\mathbb{T})\longrightarrow K^{0}(\Sigma_{p})\longrightarrow\varprojlim K^{0}(\mathbb{T})\longrightarrow 0.

(285)

Using
K^{-1}(\mathbb{T})=K^{1}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbb{Z}
with transition
(\times p)
and
K^{0}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbb{Z}
with transition
(\mathrm{id})
, we obtain

\varprojlim K^{0}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbb{Z},\qquad\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}K^{-1}(\mathbb{T})\cong\mathbf{T}_{p}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p},

(286)

hence an extension

0\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow K^{0}(\Sigma_{p})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}\longrightarrow 0.

(287)

•

For
i=1
:

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}K^{0}(\mathbb{T})\longrightarrow K^{1}(\Sigma_{p})\longrightarrow\varprojlim K^{1}(\mathbb{T})\longrightarrow 0,

(288)

and since the
K^{0}
-system is constant
(\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\mathrm{id}}\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\cdots})
,

\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}K^{0}(\mathbb{T})=0,\qquad\varprojlim K^{1}(\mathbb{T})=\varprojlim(\cdots\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z})=0,

(289)

so

K^{1}(\Sigma_{p})=0.

(290)

Finally, record the tower-visible odd
K
-group structure used as bookkeeping for completion/torsion phenomena:

K^{-1}(\Sigma_{p})\;\cong\;\bigl(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\bigr)\ \oplus\ \mathbb{Z}\ \oplus\ \mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\oplus\infty}.

(291)

(Here (
291
) is a structural decomposition statement it isolates the
p
-divisible derived-limit component
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and the pro-
(p)
completion components
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
which are invisible to naive manifold invariants.)

Appendix B
K-theory and charge quantization

Fix a prime
p
.
Let
\{X_{n},f_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
be an inverse system of compact Hausdorff spaces with surjective bonding maps

\cdots\xrightarrow{f_{n+1}}X_{n}\xrightarrow{f_{n}}X_{n-1}\xrightarrow{}\cdots\xrightarrow{f_{1}}X_{0}.

(292)

Define the tower-completed carrier as the inverse limit in
\mathrm{CompHaus}
,

X\;:=\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,(X_{n},f_{n}),\qquad X\subseteq\prod_{n\geq 0}X_{n},\quad f_{n}(x_{n})=x_{n-1}.

(293)

Fix a generalized cohomology theory
G^{\ast}(-)
on compact Hausdorff spaces, in this appendix
G^{\ast}=K^{\ast}
denotes complex topological
K
-theory.
The only nonstandard datum used below is the
tower-compatibility constraint
on background sectors:
every global datum on
X
is represented as a compatible family on the finite stages
\{X_{n}\}
.

Let
\mathrm{Prof}
denote the site of profinite sets with its canonical Grothendieck topology, and let
\mathrm{Cond}
denote condensed sets (sheaves on
\mathrm{Prof}
).
To
X
we associate its condensed avatar

\underline{X}:\mathrm{Prof}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Sets},\qquad\underline{X}(S):=\mathrm{Cont}(S,X).

(294)

For each
n
set
\underline{X}_{n}(S):=\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n})
, the maps
f_{n}
induce pullbacks
f_{n}^{\ast}:\underline{X}_{n-1}\to\underline{X}_{n}
.
Then for every profinite test object
S
,

\displaystyle\underline{X}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cont}(S,\varprojlim X_{n})\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n})=\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,\underline{X}_{n}(S),

(295)

\displaystyle\underline{X}

\displaystyle\cong\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,\underline{X}_{n}\qquad\text{in }\mathrm{Cond}.

(296)

If
\mathcal{A}
is a condensed abelian group on
X
(i.e. a sheaf of abelian groups on
\mathrm{Prof}
equipped with an
X
-module structure), its sections on the tower-defined carrier are, by definition,

\Gamma(X,\mathcal{A})\;:=\;\mathcal{A}(\underline{X})\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,\mathcal{A}(\underline{X}_{n})\;=\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\,\Gamma(X_{n},\mathcal{A}_{n}),

(297)

for the induced tower
\{\mathcal{A}_{n}\}
under pullback along
f_{n}
.
Equation (
297
) is the operational content used below “global” means “compatible family over all finite stages”.

Let
\{A_{n},f_{n}:A_{n}\to A_{n-1}\}
be an inverse system in
\mathrm{Ab}
.
Write

\mathrm{shift}:\prod_{n\geq 0}A_{n}\to\prod_{n\geq 0}A_{n},\qquad\mathrm{shift}\bigl((a_{n})_{n}\bigr)=(f_{n+1}(a_{n+1}))_{n\geq 0}.

(298)

Then the standard exact sequence defining
\varprojlim
and
\varprojlim^{1}
is

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}A_{n}\longrightarrow\prod_{n\geq 0}A_{n}\xrightarrow{\,1-\mathrm{shift}\,}\prod_{n\geq 0}A_{n}\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}A_{n}\longrightarrow 0.

(299)

Let
X=\varprojlim X_{n}
be as in (
293
). For any generalized cohomology theory
G^{\ast}
satisfying the standard hypotheses for Milnor exactness on towers of compact Hausdorff spaces, one has the Milnor short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}G^{i-1}(X_{n})\longrightarrow G^{i}(X)\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}G^{i}(X_{n})\longrightarrow 0,\qquad i\in\mathbb{Z}.

(300)

Define the degree-
p
map on the circle

S^{1}\xrightarrow{\,f\,}S^{1},\qquad f(z)=z^{p}.

(301)

Let
X_{n}=S^{1}
for all
n
, with bonding maps
f_{n}=f
, define the solenoidal carrier

\Sigma_{p}\;:=\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}(S^{1},f).

(302)

The stagewise
K
-groups and induced maps are

K^{0}(S^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z},\qquad K^{1}(S^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z},\qquad f^{\ast}|_{K^{0}}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}},\quad f^{\ast}|_{K^{1}}=\times p.

(303)

Set
A_{n}:=\mathbb{Z}
with bonding maps
\times p
, i.e. the inverse system

\cdots\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}.

(304)

Then

\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}A_{n}=\Bigl\{(a_{n})_{n}\in\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{Z}:\ a_{n}=p\,a_{n+1}\ \forall n\Bigr\}=\{0\},

(305)

and by (
299
) with
f_{n+1}=\times p
,

\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}A_{n}\;\cong\;\mathrm{coker}\Bigl(\delta:\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{Z}\to\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{Z}\Bigr),\qquad\delta((t_{n})_{n})=(t_{n}-p\,t_{n+1})_{n\geq 0}.

(306)

Introduce the
p
-adic integers and the localized rationals

\mathbb{Z}_{p}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z},\qquad\mathbb{Z}[1/p]:=\Bigl\{\frac{a}{p^{n}}:\ a\in\mathbb{Z},\ n\geq 0\Bigr\},\qquad\mathbb{Q}_{p}:=\mathrm{Frac}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

(307)

The quotient
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is the
p
-primary divisible torsion group

\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\;\cong\;\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{p^{n}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\Big/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\;\cong\;\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{p^{n}}\mathbb{Z}\Big/\mathbb{Z}\;\cong\;\mathbb{Z}[1/p]/\mathbb{Z}.

(308)

In this paper the derived-limit computation used is the standard identification

\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}\Bigl(\cdots\xleftarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}\Bigr)\;\cong\;\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p},

(309)

implemented by choosing representatives of
\mathrm{coker}(\delta)
in (
306
) via the carry-normal form associated to base-
p
expansions and mapping the resulting class to its image in
\mathbb{Z}[1/p]/\mathbb{Z}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
through (
308
).

Applying (
300
) with
G^{\ast}=K^{\ast}
and (
303
) yields:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}K^{-1}(S^{1})\longrightarrow K^{0}(\Sigma_{p})\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}K^{0}(S^{1})\longrightarrow 0,

(310)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}K^{0}(S^{1})\longrightarrow K^{1}(\Sigma_{p})\longrightarrow\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}K^{1}(S^{1})\longrightarrow 0.

(311)

Using (
303
)-(
309
) and that the constant inverse system
(\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\mathrm{id}}\mathbb{Z}\xleftarrow{\mathrm{id}}\cdots)
has
\varprojlim^{1}=0
, one obtains

\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}K^{0}(S^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z},\qquad\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}K^{-1}(S^{1})\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p},\qquad\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}K^{1}(S^{1})=0,\qquad\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n}K^{0}(S^{1})=0.

(312)

Hence the tower-completed
K
-theory satisfies the extension

0\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow K^{0}(\Sigma_{p})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}\longrightarrow 0,

(313)

and

K^{1}(\Sigma_{p})=0.

(314)

For a compact abelian group
G
, define the continuous character group

G^{\vee}\;:=\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,U(1)).

(315)

If
G=\varprojlim G_{n}
is an inverse limit of compact abelian groups with surjective bonding maps, Pontryagin duality gives

\Bigl(\varprojlim\nolimits_{n}G_{n}\Bigr)^{\vee}\;\cong\;\varinjlim\nolimits_{n}G_{n}^{\vee}.

(316)

Apply (
316
) to
G_{n}=S^{1}
with
f(z)=z^{p}
. Since
(S^{1})^{\vee}\cong\mathbb{Z}
via
\chi_{m}(z)=z^{m}
and

\chi_{m}\circ f=\chi_{pm}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad f^{\vee}:\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z},\ m\mapsto pm,

(317)

one obtains

\Sigma_{p}^{\vee}\;\cong\;\varinjlim\Bigl(\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\times p}\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\times p}\cdots\Bigr)\;\cong\;\mathbb{Z}[1/p],

(318)

with the explicit identification

[m,n]\longmapsto\frac{m}{p^{n}},\qquad(m,n)\sim(pm,n+1).

(319)

In particular, any tower-compatible
U(1)
-holonomy datum on
\Sigma_{p}
is encoded by a class in
\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
.

Define the finite-stage bookkeeping sets

\Lambda_{n}\;:=\;\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z},\qquad\Lambda_{n+1}\to\Lambda_{n}:\ (a,\bar{b}_{n+1})\mapsto(a,\bar{b}_{n+1}\!\!\!\pmod{p^{n}}),

(320)

and the inverse limit

\Lambda_{\infty}\;:=\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\Lambda_{n}\;\cong\;\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

(321)

Fix the dense embedding
\iota:\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}
induced by
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
for all
n
.
Define the
integral physical sublattice
(quantization constraint)

\Lambda_{\mathrm{phys}}\;:=\;\mathbb{Z}\oplus\iota(\mathbb{Z})\;\subset\;\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}\;\cong\;\Lambda_{\infty}.

(322)

Equivalently,
\Lambda_{\mathrm{phys}}
is the image of
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
under

\mathbb{Z}^{2}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p},\qquad(a,b)\longmapsto(a,\iota(b)).

(323)

At each finite stage
n
, the reduction map
\rho_{n}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
satisfies
\rho_{n}(\iota(\mathbb{Z}))=\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
, hence the induced image of
\Lambda_{\mathrm{phys}}
in
\Lambda_{n}
is all of
\Lambda_{n}
:

(\mathrm{id}\oplus\rho_{n})(\Lambda_{\mathrm{phys}})=\Lambda_{n},\qquad\forall n\geq 1.

(324)

Thus the selection (
322
) is a
limit constraint
on lifts to
\Lambda_{\infty}
.

Define the bookkeeping injection (finite-stage normal form) by choosing the canonical representatives

s_{n}:\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\to\{0,1,\dots,p^{n}-1\}\subset\mathbb{Z}
and setting

j_{n}:\Lambda_{n}\to\mathbb{Z}[1/p],\qquad j_{n}(a,\bar{b}):=a+\frac{s_{n}(\bar{b})}{p^{n}}.

(325)

Then
j_{n}
is injective and satisfies the compatibility relation

j_{n+1}(a,\bar{b}_{n+1})=j_{n}\bigl(a^{\prime},\bar{b}_{n}\bigr)\quad\text{whenever}\quad\bar{b}_{n}=\bar{b}_{n+1}\!\!\!\pmod{p^{n}},\ \ a^{\prime}=a+\Bigl\lfloor\frac{s_{n+1}(\bar{b}_{n+1})}{p^{n}}\Bigr\rfloor.

(326)

Moreover,

\bigcup_{n\geq 1}j_{n}(\Lambda_{n})=\mathbb{Z}[1/p].

(327)

The numerator map used for finite-stage bookkeeping is

\iota_{n}:\Lambda_{n}\to\mathbb{Z},\qquad\iota_{n}(a,\bar{b}):=p^{n}\,a+s_{n}(\bar{b}),\qquad\iota_{n}=p^{n}\cdot j_{n},

(328)

so that
\iota_{n}(\Lambda_{n})=\mathbb{Z}
and
j_{n}(\Lambda_{n})=p^{-n}\mathbb{Z}+\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\Lambda_{\infty}
be a fixed subgroup (instanton/defect test lattice) and let

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:\Lambda_{\infty}\times\mathcal{I}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}

(329)

be a bilinear pairing defining the integrality constraint. The admissible charge lattice is the subgroup

\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}\;:=\;\Bigl\{q\in\Lambda_{\infty}:\ \langle q,i\rangle=0\ \text{in }\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\ \ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}\Bigr\}.

(330)

In this work the constraint is imposed by the choice

\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}=\Lambda_{\mathrm{phys}}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{2}\subset\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p},

(331)

i.e. only integer lifts in the profinite coordinate are retained.

For the solenoidal carrier
\Sigma_{p}=\varprojlim(S^{1}\xleftarrow{z\mapsto z^{p}}S^{1}\xleftarrow{\cdots})
, the tower-completed
K
-theory contains a canonical derived-limit torsion sector
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
via (
313
) and (
314
), while the tower-compatible
U(1)
-holonomy sectors are identified with
\Sigma_{p}^{\vee}\cong\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
via (
318
), admissible charge labels are selected by the integrality constraint
\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}=\mathbb{Z}\oplus\iota(\mathbb{Z})\subset\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in (
331
), with finite-stage representatives encoded by (
325
)-(
328
).

Appendix C
Duality-group refinements and anomalies

This appendix fixes the arithmetic duality groups acting on the rank-two charge lattice and on the elliptic modulus, then derives (i) the extension of the holomorphic modular action from
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
to an antiholomorphic
GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
-action with orientation character
\det
, and (ii) the anomaly-refined
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-central extension (“Pin
+
-refinement”) required to act on fermionic determinants, condensed mathematics is operationalized by packaging congruence-level data and group actions as inverse limits of finite objects in the category of condensed groups and by formulating tower-wise pin data as compatible descent data.

Definition C.1

(Upper half-plane and period lattice)
.

Define the upper half-plane

\mathfrak{H}\;:=\;\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}:\ \Im(\tau)>0\}.

(332)

For
\tau\in\mathfrak{H}
, define the rank-two lattice

\Lambda_{\tau}\;:=\;\mathbb{Z}\omega_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\omega_{2}\subset\mathbb{C},\qquad(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=(1,\tau),

(333)

and the associated complex torus

E_{\tau}\;:=\;\mathbb{C}/\Lambda_{\tau}.

(334)

Definition C.2

(Charge lattice)
.

Fix the free abelian group

\Gamma\;:=\;\mathbb{Z}^{2},\qquad Q=\binom{p}{q}\in\Gamma,

(335)

equipped with the standard skew form

J\;:=\;\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\langle Q,Q^{\prime}\rangle\;:=\;Q^{\mathsf{T}}JQ^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}.

(336)

Lemma C.3

(Change of oriented basis
\Rightarrow
fractional linear transformation)
.

Let
\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
.
Define a new oriented basis of
\Lambda_{\tau}
by

(\omega_{1}^{\prime},\omega_{2}^{\prime})\;:=\;(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\,\gamma\;=\;(a\omega_{1}+c\omega_{2},\ b\omega_{1}+d\omega_{2}).

(337)

Then
\Lambda_{\tau}^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}\omega_{1}^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\omega_{2}^{\prime}=\Lambda_{\tau}
and the induced modulus is

\tau^{\prime}\;:=\;\frac{\omega_{2}^{\prime}}{\omega_{1}^{\prime}}\;=\;\frac{b+d\tau}{a+c\tau}\;=\;\frac{a\tau+b}{c\tau+d}.

(338)

Proof.

Using
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=(1,\tau)
,

\omega_{1}^{\prime}=a+c\tau,\qquad\omega_{2}^{\prime}=b+d\tau,

(339)

hence
\tau^{\prime}=\omega_{2}^{\prime}/\omega_{1}^{\prime}=(b+d\tau)/(a+c\tau)
.
Since
ad-bc=1
, the
\mathbb{Z}
-span of
(\omega_{1}^{\prime},\omega_{2}^{\prime})
equals that of
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
, so
E_{\tau^{\prime}}\cong E_{\tau}
by the induced complex-linear isomorphism
z\mapsto z
.
∎

Lemma C.4

(Oriented action on charges)
.

For
\gamma\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
define

Q\longmapsto Q^{\prime}:=\gamma Q.

(340)

Then
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is preserved:

\langle\gamma Q,\gamma Q^{\prime}\rangle=Q^{\mathsf{T}}\gamma^{\mathsf{T}}J\gamma Q^{\prime}=Q^{\mathsf{T}}JQ^{\prime}=\langle Q,Q^{\prime}\rangle,\qquad\gamma^{\mathsf{T}}J\gamma=J.

(341)

Proof.

Write
\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
with
ad-bc=1
and compute

\gamma^{\mathsf{T}}J\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a&c\\
b&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&ad-bc\\
-(ad-bc)&0\end{pmatrix}=J.

(342)

∎

Definition C.5

(Orientation character and extension group)
.

Define

GL(2,\mathbb{Z})=\left\{\gamma\in\mathrm{Mat}_{2\times 2}(\mathbb{Z}):\ \det(\gamma)=\pm 1\right\},\qquad\varepsilon(\gamma):=\det(\gamma)\in\{\pm 1\}.

(343)

Then

1\longrightarrow SL(2,\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow GL(2,\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\ \det\ }\{\pm 1\}\longrightarrow 1

(344)

is exact.

Lemma C.6

(Intersection form transforms with
\det
)
.

For
\gamma\in GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
,

\gamma^{\mathsf{T}}J\gamma\;=\;\det(\gamma)\,J.

(345)

Proof.

Let
\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
with
\det(\gamma)=ad-bc=\pm 1
. Then

\gamma^{\mathsf{T}}J\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a&c\\
b&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&ad-bc\\
-(ad-bc)&0\end{pmatrix}=\det(\gamma)\,J.

(346)

∎

Definition C.7

(
GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
action on
\mathfrak{H}
)
.

For
\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
define

\gamma\cdot\tau\;:=\;\begin{cases}\dfrac{a\tau+b}{c\tau+d},&\det(\gamma)=+1,\\[9.47217pt]
\dfrac{a\overline{\tau}+b}{c\overline{\tau}+d},&\det(\gamma)=-1,\end{cases}\qquad\tau\in\mathfrak{H}.

(347)

Lemma C.8

(Antiholomorphic realization for
\det=-1
)
.

If
\det(\gamma)=-1
then (
347
) is the unique extension of the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
-action (
338
) compatible with complex conjugation on the universal cover:

z\mapsto\overline{z}:\ \mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C},\qquad\overline{\Lambda_{\tau}}=\Lambda_{\overline{\tau}},\qquad E_{\tau}\xrightarrow{\ z\mapsto\overline{z}\ }\overline{E_{\tau}}\cong E_{\gamma\cdot\tau}.

(348)

Proof.

Let
\tau\in\mathfrak{H}
and
\det(\gamma)=-1
.
Start from the conjugated lattice
\overline{\Lambda_{\tau}}=\mathbb{Z}(1)\oplus\mathbb{Z}(\overline{\tau})
.
Apply the integral basis change
(1,\overline{\tau})\mapsto(1,\overline{\tau})\gamma=(a+c\overline{\tau},\,b+d\overline{\tau})
.
The ratio of new periods is

\frac{b+d\overline{\tau}}{a+c\overline{\tau}}=\frac{a\overline{\tau}+b}{c\overline{\tau}+d}

(349)

(after swapping the matrix roles as in Lemma
C.3
), which is precisely the second line of (
347
). The map
z\mapsto\overline{z}
induces an antiholomorphic isomorphism
\mathbb{C}/\Lambda_{\tau}\to\mathbb{C}/\overline{\Lambda_{\tau}}
, and the basis change identifies
\overline{\Lambda_{\tau}}
with
\Lambda_{\gamma\cdot\tau}
.
∎

Definition C.9

(
GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
action on charges)
.

For
\gamma\in GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
define

Q\longmapsto\gamma Q,\qquad Q\in\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}^{2}.

(350)

Then by Lemma
C.6
the pairing transforms by

\langle\gamma Q,\gamma Q^{\prime}\rangle=\det(\gamma)\,\langle Q,Q^{\prime}\rangle.

(351)

Definition C.10

(Condensed realization of discrete abelian groups)
.

Let
\mathrm{Prof}
be the site of profinite sets.
For a discrete abelian group
A
, define the associated condensed abelian group

\underline{A}:\mathrm{Prof}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Ab},\qquad\underline{A}(S):=\mathrm{Cont}(S,A),

(352)

where
\mathrm{Cont}(S,A)
denotes locally constant maps (equivalently continuous maps for
A
discrete).

Definition C.11

(Congruence tower and profinite completion of the charge lattice)
.

Fix
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
For each
n\geq 1
define the finite quotient

\Gamma_{n}\;:=\;\Gamma/p^{n}\Gamma\;\cong\;(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{2},\qquad r_{n+1,n}:\Gamma_{n+1}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{n}.

(353)

Define the profinite completion

\Gamma_{\!p}\;:=\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\Gamma_{n}\;\cong\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{2}.

(354)

Lemma C.12

(Condensed limit computes sections as compatible families)
.

For every profinite set
S
,

\underline{\Gamma_{\!p}}(S)=\mathrm{Cont}(S,\Gamma_{\!p})\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\mathrm{Cont}(S,\Gamma_{n})=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\underline{\Gamma_{n}}(S),

(355)

hence

\underline{\Gamma_{\!p}}\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\underline{\Gamma_{n}}\qquad\text{in }\mathrm{CondAb}.

(356)

Proof.

The inverse limit
\Gamma_{\!p}=\varprojlim\Gamma_{n}
is a profinite (hence compact Hausdorff) group, continuity of
\mathrm{Cont}(S,-)
on inverse limits of compact Hausdorff spaces yields (
355
) as an equalizer of compatible families, which is the defining universal property of the inverse limit in
\mathrm{CondAb}
, giving (
356
).
∎

Definition C.13

(Congruence tower of duality groups and condensed action)
.

Define finite groups

G_{n}\;:=\;\mathrm{Aut}(\Gamma_{n})\;\cong\;GL(2,\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}),\qquad\pi_{n+1,n}:G_{n+1}\twoheadrightarrow G_{n}.

(357)

Define the profinite group

G_{\!p}\;:=\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}G_{n}\;\cong\;GL(2,\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

(358)

and the induced condensed group

\underline{G_{\!p}}\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\underline{G_{n}}\qquad\text{in }\mathrm{CondGrp}.

(359)

The action is defined on
S
-points by evaluation:

\underline{G_{\!p}}(S)\times\underline{\Gamma_{\!p}}(S)\to\underline{\Gamma_{\!p}}(S),\qquad(g,x)\mapsto(s\mapsto g(s)\cdot x(s)).

(360)

Definition C.14

(
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-central extensions via
2
-cocycles)
.

Let
G
be a group and let
\omega:G\times G\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
satisfy the cocycle condition

\omega(g,h)+\omega(gh,k)=\omega(h,k)+\omega(g,hk),\qquad\forall g,h,k\in G,

(361)

and normalization
\omega(e,g)=\omega(g,e)=0
.
Define
\widetilde{G}_{\omega}:=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times G
with multiplication

(\epsilon,g)\cdot(\epsilon^{\prime},h):=\bigl(\epsilon+\epsilon^{\prime}+\omega(g,h),\ gh\bigr),\qquad\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2},\ g,h\in G.

(362)

Lemma C.15

(Associativity and exact sequence)
.

The product (
362
) is associative if and only if (
361
) holds, and the sequence

1\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}_{\omega}\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow 1

(363)

is exact with central kernel.

Proof.

Compute

\bigl((\epsilon,g)\cdot(\epsilon^{\prime},h)\bigr)\cdot(\epsilon^{\prime\prime},k)=\bigl(\epsilon+\epsilon^{\prime}+\omega(g,h),gh\bigr)\cdot(\epsilon^{\prime\prime},k)=\bigl(\epsilon+\epsilon^{\prime}+\epsilon^{\prime\prime}+\omega(g,h)+\omega(gh,k),\ ghk\bigr),

(364)

and

(\epsilon,g)\cdot\bigl((\epsilon^{\prime},h)\cdot(\epsilon^{\prime\prime},k)\bigr)=(\epsilon,g)\cdot\bigl(\epsilon^{\prime}+\epsilon^{\prime\prime}+\omega(h,k),hk\bigr)=\bigl(\epsilon+\epsilon^{\prime}+\epsilon^{\prime\prime}+\omega(h,k)+\omega(g,hk),\ ghk\bigr),

(365)

so associativity is equivalent to (
361
).
Exactness and centrality follow because the projection
(\epsilon,g)\mapsto g
is a homomorphism with kernel
\{(\epsilon,e)\}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
(\epsilon,e)
commutes with all elements by (
362
).
∎

Definition C.16

(Pin
+
refinement of
GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
(axiomatic form))
.

Let
G=GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
.
A
Pin
+
refinement
is a choice of a cohomology class

[\omega_{\mathrm{an}}]\in H^{2}\bigl(GL(2,\mathbb{Z});\mathbb{Z}_{2}\bigr)

(366)

and a representative cocycle
\omega_{\mathrm{an}}
determining a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-central extension

1\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{GL}(2,\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow GL(2,\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow 1,\qquad\widetilde{GL}(2,\mathbb{Z}):=\widetilde{G}_{\omega_{\mathrm{an}}}.

(367)

The class
[\omega_{\mathrm{an}}]
is fixed by the anomaly data of the fermionic functional determinant (cf.
[
10
]
).

Definition C.17

(Tower-wise pin
+
structures)
.

Let
\{M_{n},g_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
be a tower of smooth manifolds with covering maps
g_{n}:M_{n+1}\to M_{n}
.
Let
\mathrm{Pin}^{+}(M_{n})
denote the groupoid of Pin
+
-structures on
TM_{n}
and their isomorphisms, and let

g_{n}^{\ast}:\mathrm{Pin}^{+}(M_{n})\to\mathrm{Pin}^{+}(M_{n+1})

(368)

be pullback.
Define the tower-wise pin
+
groupoid by the inverse-limit condition

\mathrm{Pin}^{+}_{\mathrm{tower}}(\{M_{n}\}):=\left\{(P_{n},\phi_{n})_{n\geq 0}:\begin{array}[]{l}P_{n}\in\mathrm{Ob}\,\mathrm{Pin}^{+}(M_{n}),\\
\phi_{n}:g_{n}^{\ast}P_{n}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }P_{n+1}\end{array}\right\}\Big/\cong,

(369)

where
\cong
denotes levelwise isomorphism compatible with the
\phi_{n}
.

Lemma C.18

(Tower-wise pin
+
is a descent object)
.

The set of isomorphism classes of (
369
) is the equalizer of the two pullback maps on classes:

\pi_{0}\mathrm{Pin}^{+}_{\mathrm{tower}}(\{M_{n}\})\;\cong\;\ker\!\left(\prod_{n\geq 0}\pi_{0}\mathrm{Pin}^{+}(M_{n})\rightrightarrows\prod_{n\geq 0}\pi_{0}\mathrm{Pin}^{+}(M_{n+1})\right),

(370)

where the two arrows are
(P_{n})\mapsto(g_{n}^{\ast}P_{n})
and
(P_{n})\mapsto(P_{n+1})
.

Proof.

By definition, an object of
\mathrm{Pin}^{+}_{\mathrm{tower}}(\{M_{n}\})
is precisely a family
(P_{n})
equipped with identifications
g_{n}^{\ast}P_{n}\cong P_{n+1}
, passing to isomorphism classes yields the equalizer condition (
370
).
∎

Definition C.19

(Compatibility requirement for an action on fermionic determinants)
.

Let
G\subseteq GL(2,\mathbb{Z})
be a duality group acting on background data.
A tower-wise fermionic action requires:

\text{a lift }G\to\widetilde{GL}(2,\mathbb{Z})\ \text{ and a functorial action }\widetilde{GL}(2,\mathbb{Z})\curvearrowright\mathrm{Pin}^{+}_{\mathrm{tower}}(\{M_{n}\})

(371)

compatible with pullback along the tower maps, i.e. commuting with the equalizer description (
370
).

The holomorphic elliptic datum
(E_{\tau},\Lambda_{\tau})
supports the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
action (
338
) and the symplectic charge action (
340
) preserving (
336
), while the inclusion of orientation reversal extends the modulus action to (
347
) and twists the pairing by
\det
as in (
351
), acting on fermionic determinants requires a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-central extension (
367
) and tower-compatible pin
+
descent data (
369
)-(
370
).

Appendix D
p
-independence and adelic completion

Fix notation for the prime-indexed tower-completed carriers and their perfectoid enhancements, and derive (i) a precise tower-stability criterion expressing that low-energy data is insensitive to refinement level for each prime, (ii) a corresponding
p
-independence
condition isolating all possible sources of apparent
p
-dependence as choices in comparison data and in the selection of admissible global sectors, and (iii) an adelic assembly formalism in the category of condensed objects via restricted products, identifying the canonical global object which packages all primes together with the Archimedean place.

Definition D.1

(
p
-tower of circles and tower limit)
.

Let
p
be prime and set
S^{1}:=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\ |z|=1\}
.
Define the endomorphism
f_{p}:S^{1}\to S^{1}
by

f_{p}(z)=z^{p}.

(372)

Define the inverse system in
\mathrm{CompHausGrp}
by
S^{1}_{n}:=S^{1}
and bonding maps
f_{p}:S^{1}_{n+1}\to S^{1}_{n}
, and its inverse limit

\Sigma_{p}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\bigl(S^{1}_{n},f_{p}\bigr)=\Bigl\{(z_{0},z_{1},\dots)\in\prod_{n\geq 0}S^{1}:\ z_{n}=z_{n+1}^{p}\ \forall n\Bigr\}.

(373)

Definition D.2

(Perfectoid circle over a
p
-adic base field)
.

Let
K_{p}:=\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
(cyclotomic extension, fixed once and for all), with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K_{p}}
.
Let

R_{p}:=K_{p}\langle X^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle,\qquad R_{p}^{+}:=\mathcal{O}_{K_{p}}\langle X^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle,

(374)

where
K_{p}\langle X^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle
denotes the
p
-adic completion of
K_{p}[X^{\pm 1/p^{n}}:n\geq 0]
in the Gauss norm.
Define the perfectoid circle

S^{1}_{f,p}:=\mathrm{Spa}(R_{p},R_{p}^{+}).

(375)

Definition D.3

(Perfectoidness and tilt)
.

Impose perfectoidness by Frobenius-surjectivity on the mod-
p
reduction:

\varphi:\ R_{p}^{+}/p\longrightarrow R_{p}^{+}/p,\qquad x\longmapsto x^{p},\qquad\varphi\ \text{surjective}.

(376)

Define the tilt rings by inverse limits along Frobenius:

R_{p}^{\flat}:=\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}R_{p},\qquad(R_{p}^{+})^{\flat}:=\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}R_{p}^{+},

(377)

and the tilted perfectoid circle

(S^{1}_{f,p})^{\flat}:=\mathrm{Spa}\bigl(R_{p}^{\flat},(R_{p}^{+})^{\flat}\bigr).

(378)

Definition D.4

(Condensed avatar and tower evaluation)
.

Let
\mathrm{Prof}
be the site of profinite sets.
For any compact Hausdorff space
X
, define its condensed avatar

\underline{X}:\mathrm{Prof}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Sets},\qquad\underline{X}(S):=\mathrm{Cont}(S,X).

(379)

Lemma D.5

(Inverse limits computed on profinite test objects)
.

For each profinite
S
and each prime
p
,

\underline{\Sigma}_{p}(S)=\mathrm{Cont}(S,\Sigma_{p})\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Cont}(S,S^{1})\qquad\text{with transition }\psi\mapsto f_{p}\circ\psi.

(380)

Equivalently, in
\mathrm{Cond}
,

\underline{\Sigma}_{p}\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\underline{S^{1}}.

(381)

Proof.

A continuous map
\phi:S\to\Sigma_{p}\subseteq\prod_{n\geq 0}S^{1}
is equivalent to a family
\phi_{n}:=\pi_{n}\circ\phi\in\mathrm{Cont}(S,S^{1})
satisfying
\phi_{n}=f_{p}\circ\phi_{n+1}
(by the defining equalizer condition in (
373
)).
Conversely, any compatible family
(\phi_{n})_{n\geq 0}
defines
\phi(s):=(\phi_{n}(s))_{n\geq 0}\in\Sigma_{p}
.
This identifies
\mathrm{Cont}(S,\Sigma_{p})
with the inverse limit of the sets
\mathrm{Cont}(S,S^{1})
.
∎

Definition D.6

(Tower-wise fields as descent data)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf (or condensed abelian group) of field-values on
\underline{\Sigma}_{p}
.
Define its sections on the tower carrier by the universal property of the inverse limit:

\Gamma(\underline{\Sigma}_{p},\mathcal{F}):=\mathcal{F}(\underline{\Sigma}_{p})\;\cong\;\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{F}(\underline{S^{1}})\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}\Gamma(\underline{S^{1}},\mathcal{F}_{n}),

(382)

where
\mathcal{F}_{n}
denotes the pullback of
\mathcal{F}
to stage
n
.

Definition D.7

(Finite stages and pullback maps)
.

For each
n\geq 0
and prime
p
, let
S^{1}(n,p)
denote the degree-
p^{n}
cover of
S^{1}
and let

g_{n+1,n}^{(p)}:S^{1}(n{+}1,p)\longrightarrow S^{1}(n,p)

(383)

be the degree-
p
covering map.

Definition D.8

(Cutoff-truncated state spaces (axiomatic))
.

Fix a cutoff
\Lambda>0
.
Assume an assignment

(n,p)\longmapsto\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n,p)}

(384)

into Hilbert spaces, together with bonding morphisms induced by pullback along (
383
):

U^{(p)}_{n+1,n}:\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n,p)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n+1,p)}.

(385)

Definition D.9

(Tower-stability at fixed cutoff)
.

The system
\{\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n,p)},U^{(p)}_{n+1,n}\}
is
stable
if there exists
n_{0}=n_{0}(\Lambda,p)
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
the iterated bonding maps

U^{(p)}_{n,n_{0}}:=U^{(p)}_{n,n-1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{(p)}_{n_{0}+1,n_{0}}:\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n_{0},p)}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n,p)}

(386)

are isomorphisms.

Definition D.10

(
p
-independence at fixed cutoff)
.

Let
\Lambda
be fixed.
A family of stable systems
\{\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n,p)}\}_{p}
is
p
-independent
if for every pair of primes
(p,p^{\prime})
there exists an identification of stabilized limits

\Phi_{\Lambda}^{p,p^{\prime}}:\ \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(\infty,p)}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(\infty,p^{\prime})},\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(\infty,p)}:=\varinjlim_{n\geq n_{0}(\Lambda,p)}\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n,p)},

(387)

such that any tower-defined observable
O_{\Lambda}
factors through
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(\infty,p)}
and is invariant under
\Phi_{\Lambda}^{p,p^{\prime}}
:

O_{\Lambda}^{(p)}=O_{\Lambda}^{(p^{\prime})}\circ\Phi_{\Lambda}^{p,p^{\prime}}.

(388)

Definition D.11

(Comparison data dependence)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{p}
denote the set of auxiliary choices entering a comparison/untilt/analytification procedure from (
378
) to complex elliptic data, including (at minimum) a choice of embedding

\iota_{p}:\ K_{p}^{\flat}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}.

(389)

Any numerical invariant defined purely by
\mathcal{C}_{p}
is a function

I_{p}^{\mathrm{cmp}}:\ \mathcal{C}_{p}\to\mathcal{T}

(390)

to some target
\mathcal{T}
and is,
a priori
, not canonically comparable across
p
.

Definition D.12

(Admissible sublattice selection)
.

Let
B_{p}
be a prime-dependent bookkeeping group of global sectors.
An admissible lattice selection is a choice of subgroup

\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}\ \subseteq\ B_{p}

(391)

which is declared physically realized.
A
p
-independent selection is a family
\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}^{(p)}\subseteq B_{p}
such that for all
p,p^{\prime}
there exist identifications
\psi^{p,p^{\prime}}:B_{p}\to B_{p^{\prime}}
with
\psi^{p,p^{\prime}}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}^{(p)})=\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}^{(p^{\prime})}
.

Lemma D.13

(Integral intersection criterion for localization-type bookkeeping)
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{Q}
and suppose
x\in\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
for every prime
p
.
Then
x\in\mathbb{Z}
, equivalently

\bigcap_{p\ \mathrm{prime}}\ \mathbb{Z}[1/p]=\mathbb{Z}.

(392)

Proof.

Write
x=a/b
in lowest terms with
a\in\mathbb{Z}
,
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\gcd(a,b)=1
.
If
x\in\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
, then
b
has no prime divisor different from
p
, hence for all primes
p
, the set of prime divisors of
b
is contained in
\{p\}
, forcing
b=1
.
Thus
x\in\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

Definition D.14

(Congruence-level completion vs global completion)
.

Let
\Gamma
be an arithmetic group (e.g.
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
) and let
\Gamma(p^{n})
denote its principal congruence subgroups.
Define the prime-local profinite completion

\Gamma_{p}:=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}\Gamma/\Gamma(p^{n})

(393)

and the full profinite completion

\widehat{\Gamma}:=\varprojlim_{N\geq 1}\Gamma/\Gamma(N)\ \cong\ \prod_{p}\Gamma_{p}.

(394)

A single-prime tower fixes
\Gamma_{p}
, reconstructing the diagonal copy of
\Gamma
inside
\widehat{\Gamma}
is a separate global problem.

Definition D.15

(Restricted product as filtered colimit in
\mathrm{Cond}
)
.

Let
\{G_{v}\}_{v\in V}
be locally compact groups and let
K_{v}\subseteq G_{v}
be open compact subgroups for all but finitely many
v
.
Define the restricted product

\prod\nolimits_{v\in V}^{\prime}(G_{v},K_{v}):=\left\{(g_{v})_{v}\in\prod_{v}G_{v}:\ g_{v}\in K_{v}\text{ for all but finitely many }v\right\}.

(395)

Equivalently (and functorially in
\mathrm{Cond}
), for
V
countable,

\prod\nolimits_{v\in V}^{\prime}(G_{v},K_{v})\;\cong\;\varinjlim_{S\subset V\ \mathrm{finite}}\left(\ \prod_{v\in S}G_{v}\ \times\ \prod_{v\notin S}K_{v}\ \right),

(396)

where the transition maps enlarge
S
by inserting identity inclusions
K_{v}\hookrightarrow G_{v}
.

Definition D.16

(Adeles and profinite integers)
.

Let
V=\{\infty\}\cup\{\text{primes }p\}
with
G_{\infty}=\mathbb{R}
and
G_{p}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
K_{p}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Define the adele ring and profinite integers by

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{R}\times\prod\nolimits_{p}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\;\cong\;\left\{(x_{\infty},(x_{p})_{p}):\ x_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R},\ x_{p}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p},\ x_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\ \text{for a.a.\ }p\right\},

(397)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle:=\prod_{p}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

(398)

Definition D.17

(Adelic tower carrier as a condensed object (construction problem))
.

For each prime
p
, let
\Sigma_{p}
be the tower limit (
373
).
Let
G_{\infty}:=S^{1}
and, for each prime
p
, set
G_{p}:=\Sigma_{p}
with
K_{p}:=\Sigma_{p}
.
Define the adelic tower carrier as the restricted product (which here coincides with the product but is presented in restricted-product form for functorial compatibility with (
397
))

\Sigma_{\mathbb{A}}:=S^{1}\times\prod\nolimits_{p}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{p},\Sigma_{p})\;\cong\;S^{1}\times\prod_{p}\Sigma_{p},

(399)

viewed as a condensed group via (
379
).

Problem 1

(Adelic invariants (explicit computation target))
.

Compute, for
\Sigma_{\mathbb{A}}
, the intrinsic dual and generalized cohomology invariants:

\Sigma_{\mathbb{A}}^{\vee}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{A}},S^{1}),\qquad K^{\ast}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{A}}),

(400)

and identify the diagonal sublattice selected by the admissibility condition (
391
) via the intersection criterion (
392
).

Problem 2

(Tower-stability theoremization)
.

Prove tower-stability (
386
) for a specified class of cutoff-truncated constructions
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{(n,p)}
, and determine the subset of protected quantities
O_{\Lambda}
for which (
388
) holds uniformly in
p
.

Problem 3

(Prime comparison test)
.

For
p\neq p^{\prime}
, compare prime-dependent bookkeeping groups
B_{p}
and
B_{p^{\prime}}
and show that, after imposing a fixed admissible sublattice selection
\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}^{(p)}
, all tower-stable observables satisfy (
388
) equivalently, show that any residual dependence on
p
factors through the choice of
\mathcal{C}_{p}
and is eliminated by a
p
-independent matching condition.

Problem 4

(Varying-fibration congruence refinement)
.

For a base
B
with discriminant
\Delta
, define prime-local monodromy refinements

\rho_{p}:\pi_{1}(B\setminus\Delta)\to\Gamma_{p}

(401)

for
\Gamma_{p}
as in (
393
), and formulate the adelic compatibility condition as the existence of a single map

\rho_{\mathbb{A}}:\pi_{1}(B\setminus\Delta)\to\widehat{\Gamma}\cong\prod_{p}\Gamma_{p}

(402)

whose projections are the
\rho_{p}
.

Problem 5

(Boundary test for
p
-independence)
.

Given a boundary tower carrier
\Sigma_{p}
for each prime and its condensed sheaf of global sectors
\mathcal{F}_{p}
, determine whether the induced restricted-product boundary carrier
\Sigma_{\mathbb{A}}
with sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{A}}:=\boxtimes_{v}\mathcal{F}_{v}
detects any
p
-dependence after imposing a
p
-independent admissibility selection
\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}
.

The constructions above explicitly isolate the
p
-dependence of tower-defined objects into (a) prime-indexed tower/power-Frobenius structure (
372
)-(
377
) and associated choice-dependent comparison data (
389
), and (b) prime-dependent bookkeeping groups
B_{p}
together with a
separately imposed
admissible sublattice selection (
391
), tower-stability is encoded by the stabilization isomorphisms (
386
) and
p
-independence by the existence of identifications (
387
) satisfying (
388
), while adelic assembly is implemented as a restricted product in condensed mathematics via (
396
)-(
397
) and the global completion formalism (
394
).

Appendix E
Varying
\tau
and the comparison axiom

The statements below are consequences of the modular map
\underline{\tau}
(equivalently
(\rho,\tilde{\tau})
)
and the associated elliptic fibration
E(\underline{\tau})\to B^{\times}
from Definition (
7.6
).
Throughout,
p
is a fixed prime,
B
is a complex manifold,
\Delta\subset B
is an analytic hypersurface,
B^{\times}:=B\setminus\Delta
, and all (co)homology is singular with integer coefficients unless specified, we write
\mathbb{H}:=\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}:\Im\tau>0\}
. We use condensed sets only as a
proof technology
to encode tower limits and maps into target moduli spaces by explicit sheaf conditions on the profinite site, and we mark every point at which the argument uses (and would fail without) the Comparison Axiom.

Definition E.1

(Profinite site and condensed sets)
.

Let
\mathrm{Prof}
denote the category of profinite sets. A family
\{S_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{Prof}
is a covering family if the induced map
\coprod_{i\in I}S_{i}\to S
is surjective. A condensed set is a functor
F:\mathrm{Prof}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Sets}
such that for every covering family
\{S_{i}\to S\}
the sequence

F(S)\longrightarrow\prod_{i}F(S_{i})\rightrightarrows\prod_{i,j}F(S_{i}\times_{S}S_{j})

(403)

is an equalizer.

Definition E.2

(Condensed avatar of a compact Hausdorff space)
.

If
Y
is a compact Hausdorff topological space, define
\underline{Y}:\mathrm{Prof}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Sets}
by

\underline{Y}(S):=\mathrm{Cont}(S,Y),

(404)

and for a continuous map
f:Y\to Y^{\prime}
define
\underline{f}:\underline{Y}\to\underline{Y^{\prime}}
by
\underline{f}(g):=f\circ g
for
g\in\mathrm{Cont}(S,Y)
.

Lemma E.3

(Sheaf condition for
\underline{Y}
)
.

For every compact Hausdorff space
Y
, the functor
\underline{Y}
of (
404
) is a condensed set in the sense of Definition
E.1
.

Proof.

Fix a covering family
\{S_{i}\to S\}
in
\mathrm{Prof}
and write
\pi_{i}:S_{i}\to S
for the structure maps. Let

(g_{i})_{i}\in\prod_{i}\underline{Y}(S_{i})=\prod_{i}\mathrm{Cont}(S_{i},Y)

(405)

be a family satisfying equality in the overlap terms of (
403
), i.e.

g_{i}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1}=g_{j}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{2}\qquad\text{as maps }S_{i}\times_{S}S_{j}\to Y\ \ \text{for all }i,j.

(406)

Define
g:S\to Y
set-theoretically as follows. For
s\in S
, choose
i
and
t\in S_{i}
with
\pi_{i}(t)=s
(possible since
\coprod_{i}S_{i}\to S
is surjective) and set

g(s):=g_{i}(t).

(407)

To check well-definedness, suppose also
\pi_{j}(t^{\prime})=s
, then
(t,t^{\prime})\in S_{i}\times_{S}S_{j}
and (
406
) gives

g_{i}(t)=g_{i}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}(t,t^{\prime}))=g_{j}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}(t,t^{\prime}))=g_{j}(t^{\prime}),

(408)

so (
407
) is independent of the choice. We now prove continuity of
g
. Let
U\subseteq Y
be open. For each
i
we have
g_{i}^{-1}(U)\subseteq S_{i}
open and

g^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{i}\pi_{i}\bigl(g_{i}^{-1}(U)\bigr).

(409)

Since
\pi_{i}
is continuous,
\pi_{i}(g_{i}^{-1}(U))
is compact in
S
, hence closed. To show (
409
) is open it suffices (because
S
is compact Hausdorff and profinite) to show
g^{-1}(U)
is a union of clopen subsets. For each
i
,
g_{i}^{-1}(U)
is open in the profinite set
S_{i}
, hence a union of clopen subsets, write
g_{i}^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{\alpha}C_{i,\alpha}
with each
C_{i,\alpha}\subseteq S_{i}
clopen. Then
\pi_{i}(C_{i,\alpha})
is compact, hence closed, moreover
\pi_{i}(C_{i,\alpha})
is also open because
\pi_{i}
is a quotient map between compact Hausdorff spaces and
C_{i,\alpha}
is saturated for the equivalence relation generated by the cover (this saturation is exactly (
406
) translated to subsets), so
\pi_{i}(C_{i,\alpha})
is clopen. Using (
409
) we obtain
g^{-1}(U)
as a union of clopen sets, hence open. Thus
g
is continuous. Uniqueness is immediate if
h:S\to Y
satisfies
h\circ\pi_{i}=g_{i}
for all
i
, then for any
s\in S
and any
t\in S_{i}
with
\pi_{i}(t)=s
one has
h(s)=h(\pi_{i}(t))=g_{i}(t)=g(s)
. Therefore
\underline{Y}
satisfies the equalizer condition (
403
).
∎

Definition E.4

(Tower limit as a condensed object)
.

Let
\{X_{n},f_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
be an inverse system in compact Hausdorff spaces with continuous bonding maps
f_{n}:X_{n+1}\to X_{n}
. Define the topological inverse limit

X:=\varprojlim_{n}X_{n}:=\Bigl\{(x_{n})_{n\geq 0}\in\prod_{n\geq 0}X_{n}:\ f_{n}(x_{n+1})=x_{n}\ \forall n\Bigr\}

(410)

with the subspace topology. Define its condensed avatar
\underline{X}
by (
404
), and define the condensed inverse limit
\varprojlim_{n}\underline{X_{n}}
as the objectwise limit in
\mathrm{Cond}
:

\Bigl(\varprojlim_{n}\underline{X_{n}}\Bigr)(S):=\varprojlim_{n}\underline{X_{n}}(S)=\Bigl\{(\phi_{n})\in\prod_{n}\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n}):\ f_{n}\circ\phi_{n+1}=\phi_{n}\ \forall n\Bigr\}.

(411)

Lemma E.5

(Inverse limits commute with
\underline{(\cdot)}
on profinite test objects)
.

For every profinite set
S
there is a canonical bijection

\mathrm{Cont}(S,X)\ \cong\ \varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n}),

(412)

natural in
S
, hence an isomorphism of condensed sets
\underline{X}\cong\varprojlim_{n}\underline{X_{n}}
.

Proof.

Let
S
be profinite. Given
\Phi\in\mathrm{Cont}(S,X)
, write
\mathrm{pr}_{n}:X\to X_{n}
for the canonical projections and define
\phi_{n}:=\mathrm{pr}_{n}\circ\Phi\in\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n})
. Then for each
n
we have
f_{n}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{n+1}=\mathrm{pr}_{n}
on
X
by the defining relations in (
410
), hence

f_{n}\circ\phi_{n+1}=f_{n}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{n+1}\circ\Phi=\mathrm{pr}_{n}\circ\Phi=\phi_{n},

(413)

so
(\phi_{n})_{n}\in\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n})
. This defines a map

\mathrm{Cont}(S,X)\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n}),\qquad\Phi\longmapsto(\mathrm{pr}_{n}\circ\Phi)_{n}.

(414)

Conversely, given a compatible family
(\phi_{n})_{n}\in\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n})
, define a map
\Phi:S\to\prod_{n}X_{n}
by
\Phi(s):=(\phi_{n}(s))_{n\geq 0}
. Compatibility
f_{n}\circ\phi_{n+1}=\phi_{n}
implies
\Phi(s)\in X\subseteq\prod_{n}X_{n}
for every
s
, so
\Phi
lands in
X
and defines
\Phi:S\to X
. Continuity of
\Phi:S\to X
follows because the product map
S\to\prod_{n}X_{n}
is continuous (each coordinate is continuous) and
X
has the subspace topology. This defines the inverse map

\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{Cont}(S,X_{n})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cont}(S,X),\qquad(\phi_{n})_{n}\longmapsto\Bigl(s\mapsto(\phi_{n}(s))_{n}\Bigr).

(415)

Composing (
414
) and (
415
) yields the identity in both directions by inspection of components, hence (
412
). Naturality in
S
follows because both constructions are by composition with maps
S^{\prime}\to S
and projections
\mathrm{pr}_{n}
.
∎

Definition E.6

(Fibred tower-completed carrier as a condensed morphism)
.

Let
\pi_{n}:X_{n}\to B^{\times}
be continuous maps such that
\pi_{n}\circ f_{n}=\pi_{n+1}
for all
n
. Define
\pi:X\to B^{\times}
by
\pi((x_{n})):=\pi_{n}(x_{n})
, this is well-defined because

\pi_{n}(x_{n})=\pi_{n}(f_{n}(x_{n+1}))=\pi_{n+1}(x_{n+1}),

(416)

and continuity follows because
\pi=\pi_{0}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{0}
. Applying
\underline{(\cdot)}
yields a morphism of condensed sets
\underline{\pi}:\underline{X}\to\underline{B^{\times}}
.

Definition E.7

(Elliptic fibration input from Definition (
7.6
))
.

Let
\underline{\tau}:B^{\times}\to\mathcal{M}_{1,1}

be the modular map of Definition (
7.6
), and let

\varpi:E(\underline{\tau})\to B^{\times}

(417)

denote the associated smooth elliptic fibration (the pullback of the universal elliptic curve), equipped with
its canonical Hodge line (relative holomorphic one-form)
\Omega\in H^{0}(E(\underline{\tau}),\Omega^{1}_{E/B^{\times}})
.
All constructions and proofs in this appendix use only the existence of
(E(\underline{\tau}),\Omega)
as part of the
varying-
\tau
background data (Definition (
7.6
) / Appendix (
F
)), and do not assume any additional “elliptic-output”
axiom.

Definition E.8

(Homology local system and symplectic basis)
.

Assume
(CA)
. For
b\in B^{\times}
write
E_{b}:=\varpi^{-1}(b)
and
\Omega_{b}:=\Omega|_{E_{b}}
. Fix a basepoint
b_{0}\in B^{\times}
and fix an oriented
\mathbb{Z}
-basis
(A_{b_{0}},B_{b_{0}})
of
H_{1}(E_{b_{0}},\mathbb{Z})
with intersection number
A_{b_{0}}\cdot B_{b_{0}}=+1
.

Lemma E.9

(Ehresmann triviality and Gauss-Manin transport)
.

Assume
(CA)
. For every
b\in B^{\times}
there exists a neighborhood
U\ni b
and a
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism
\Phi_{U}:\varpi^{-1}(U)\to U\times E_{b}
such that
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ\Phi_{U}=\varpi
, in particular,
H_{1}(E_{b^{\prime}},\mathbb{Z})
forms a rank-
2
local system on
B^{\times}
and parallel transport along any path in
B^{\times}
is defined and preserves the intersection pairing.

Proof.

Since
\varpi:E\to B^{\times}
is a proper submersion by
(CA)
, Ehresmann’s theorem applies, yielding local
C^{\infty}
triviality as stated. The local system statement follows because for any
b^{\prime}\in U
the identification
E_{b^{\prime}}\cong E_{b}
induced by
\Phi_{U}
identifies
H_{1}(E_{b^{\prime}},\mathbb{Z})
with
H_{1}(E_{b},\mathbb{Z})
, and changes of trivialization on overlaps are diffeomorphisms of the fiber, hence act by
\mathbb{Z}
-module automorphisms preserving the intersection form.
∎

Definition E.10

(Periods and period ratio)
.

Assume
(CA)
. For
b\in B^{\times}
and a transported choice of cycles
(A_{b},B_{b})
obtained from
(A_{b_{0}},B_{b_{0}})
by Gauss-Manin transport, define

\omega_{A}(b):=\int_{A_{b}}\Omega_{b},\qquad\omega_{B}(b):=\int_{B_{b}}\Omega_{b},

(418)

and define the period ratio

\tau(b):=\frac{\omega_{A}(b)}{\omega_{B}(b)}

(419)

whenever
\omega_{B}(b)\neq 0
.

Proposition E.11

(Conditional holomorphicity of periods)
.

Assume
(CA)
. Let
U\subset B^{\times}
be simply connected. Fix cycles
(A_{b},B_{b})
on fibers
E_{b}
